Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.\n\n(End of file - total 36 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). 24: 	if !n.usesNetnsNode() {
25: 		return nil
26: 	}
27: 
28: 	n.netnsNode = new(clabnodes.NetnsHolder)
29: 
30: 	return n.netnsNode.Init(n.newNetnsConfig(), clabnodes.WithRuntime(n.GetRuntime()))
31: }

33: func (n *srl) newNetnsConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
34: 	labels := maps.Clone(n.Cfg.Labels)
35: 
36: 	if labels == nil {
37: 		labels = map[string]string{}
38: 	}
39:

41: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeName] = shortName
42: 
43: 	longName := n.Cfg.LongName + "-netns"
44: 	labels[clabconstants.LongName] = longName
45: 
46: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeType] = netnsNodeType
47: 	labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] = "true"
48: 
49: 	labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName] = n.Cfg.ShortName
50: 	labels[clabconstants.RootNodeLongName] = n.Cfg.LongName
51:

53: 		ShortName:             shortName,
54: 		LongName:              longName,
55: 		Hostname:              n.Cfg.GetHostname(),
56: 		Fqdn:                  n.Cfg.Fqdn,

85: 		Labels:                labels,
86: 		Runtime:               n.Cfg.Runtime,
87: 		ResultingPortBindings: n.Cfg.ResultingPortBindings,
88: 	}
89: }
90: 
91: // containerConfig returns the config of the SR Linux container attached to the holder's
92: // network namespace. The runtime rejects network settings for such containers, as they
93: // belong to the holder.
94: func (n *srl) containerConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
95: 	cfg := *n.Cfg
96: 	cfg.NetworkMode = "container:" + n.netnsNode.GetShortName()
97: 	cfg.DNS = nil
98: 	cfg.ExtraHosts = nil
99: 	cfg.MacAddress = ""
100: 	cfg.PortBindings = nil
101: 	cfg.PortSet = nil
102: 
103: 	return &cfg
104: }
105: 
106: // linkNetns points the node's netns symlink to the holder, as the namespace path of the
107: // SR Linux container changes whenever the container restarts.
108: func (n *srl) linkNetns(ctx context.Context) error {
109: 	nsPath, err := n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
110: 	if err != nil {
111: 		return err
112: 	}
113: 
114: 	return clabutils.LinkContainerNS(nsPath, n.Cfg.LongName)
115: }
116: 
117: // deployedWithoutNetnsNode reports whether the node's container runs without a holder: it keeps
118: // its own network namespace, as a node deployed by a containerlab before the holder does until
119: // it is recreated.
120: func (n *srl) deployedWithoutNetnsNode(ctx context.Context) bool {
121: 	return n.netnsNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound &&
122: 		n.GetContainerStatus(ctx) != clabruntime.NotFound
123: }
124: 
125: // GetNSPath retrieves the Namespace Path.
126: func (n *srl) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
127: 	if n.netnsNode == nil || n.deployedWithoutNetnsNode(ctx) {
128: 		return n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	return n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
132: }
133: 
134: // DeleteNetnsSymlink deletes the symlink file created for the container netns.
135: func (n *srl) DeleteNetnsSymlink() error {
136: 	if n.netnsNode == nil {
137: 		return n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink()
138: 	}
139: 
140: 	return errors.Join(n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink(), n.netnsNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())

97: func TestContainerConfigUsesNetnsNode(t *testing.T) {
98: 	cfg := newSRLTestConfig(t)
99: 	cfg.DNS = &clabtypes.DNSConfig{Servers: []string{"1.1.1.1"}}
100: 	cfg.ExtraHosts = []string{"srl2:172.20.20.3"}
101: 	cfg.MacAddress = "02:00:00:00:00:01"
102: 	cfg.PortSet = nat.PortSet{"443/tcp": struct{}{}}
103: 	cfg.PortBindings = nat.PortMap{"443/tcp": []nat.PortBinding{{HostPort: "50443"}}}
104: 	n := newSRLTestNode(t, cfg, nil)
105: 
106: 	got := n.containerConfig()
107: 
108: 	assert.Equal(t, "container:"+testNetnsShort, got.NetworkMode)
109: 	assert.Nil(t, got.DNS)
110: 	assert.Nil(t, got.ExtraHosts)
111: 	assert.Empty(t, got.MacAddress)
112: 	assert.Nil(t, got.PortBindings)
113: 	assert.Nil(t, got.PortSet)
114: 	assert.Equal(t, n.Cfg.Entrypoint, got.Entrypoint)
115: 	assert.Equal(t, n.Cfg.Cmd, got.Cmd)
116: 	assert.Equal(t, "bar", got.Env["FOO"])
117: 
118: 	// the node config keeps the network settings, they are applied to the holder
119: 	assert.Empty(t, n.Cfg.NetworkMode)
120: 	assert.Equal(t, []string{"srl2:172.20.20.3"}, n.Cfg.ExtraHosts)
121: 	assert.NotNil(t, n.Cfg.DNS)
122: }

126: 	rt := clabmocksmockruntime.NewMockContainerRuntime(gomock.NewController(t))
127: 	n := newSRLTestNode(t, newSRLTestConfig(t), rt)
128:

132: 		NetworkName: "clab",
133: 		NetworkSettings: clabruntime.GenericMgmtIPs{

135: 			IPv4pLen: 24,
136: 			IPv6addr: "3fff:172:20:20::10",
137: 			IPv6pLen: 64,
138: 		},
139: 		Ports: ports,
140: 	}

146: 		{FilterType: "name", Match: testNetnsName},

153: 	assert.Equal(t, holder.NetworkName, got[0].NetworkName)
154: 	assert.Equal(t, holder.NetworkSettings, got[0].NetworkSettings)
155: 	assert.Equal(t, ports, got[0].Ports)
156: }

16: // usesNetnsNode reports whether the node runs in the network namespace of a holder container.
17: // Nodes with a user-defined network mode keep the network namespace of their own container.
18: func (n *srl) usesNetnsNode() bool {
19: 	return n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "" && !n.Cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased
20: }
21: 
22: func (n *srl) initNetnsNode() error {
23: 	if !n.usesNetnsNode() {
24: 		return nil
25: 	}
26: 
27: 	n.netnsNode = new(clabnodes.NetnsHolder)
28: 
29: 	return n.netnsNode.Init(n.newNetnsConfig(), clabnodes.WithRuntime(n.GetRuntime()))
30: }
31: 
32: func (n *srl) newNetnsConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
33: 	labels := maps.Clone(n.Cfg.Labels)
34: 
35: 	if labels == nil {
36: 		labels = map[string]string{}
37: 	}
38: 
39: 	shortName := n.Cfg.ShortName + "-netns"
40: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeName] = shortName
41: 
42: 	longName := n.Cfg.LongName + "-netns"
43: 	labels[clabconstants.LongName] = longName
44: 
45: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeType] = netnsNodeType
46: 	labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] = "true"
47: 
48: 	labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName] = n.Cfg.ShortName

90: // containerConfig returns the config of the SR Linux container attached to the holder's
91: // network namespace. The runtime rejects network settings for such containers, as they
92: // belong to the holder.
93: func (n *srl) containerConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
94: 	cfg := *n.Cfg
95: 	cfg.NetworkMode = "container:" + n.netnsNode.GetShortName()
96: 	cfg.DNS = nil
97: 	cfg.ExtraHosts = nil
98: 	cfg.MacAddress = ""
99: 	cfg.PortBindings = nil
100: 	cfg.PortSet = nil
101: 
102: 	return &cfg
103: }
104: 
105: // linkNetns points the node's netns symlink to the holder, as the namespace path of the
106: // SR Linux container changes whenever the container restarts.
107: func (n *srl) linkNetns(ctx context.Context) error {
108: 	nsPath, err := n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
109: 	if err != nil {
110: 		return err
111: 	}
112: 
113: 	return clabutils.LinkContainerNS(nsPath, n.Cfg.LongName)
114: }
115: 
116: // GetNSPath retrieves the Namespace Path.
117: func (n *srl) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
118: 	if n.netnsNode == nil {
119: 		return n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
120: 	}
121: 
122: 	return n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
123: }
124: 
125: // DeleteNetnsSymlink deletes the symlink file created for the container netns.
126: func (n *srl) DeleteNetnsSymlink() error {
127: 	if n.netnsNode == nil {
128: 		return n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink()
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	return errors.Join(n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink(), n.netnsNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
132: }

err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tif err := n.addOverlayCLIConfig(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// once default and overlay config is added, we can commit the config\n\tif err := n.commitConfig(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\treturn n.generateCheckpoint(ctx)\n}\n\nfunc (n *srl) SaveConfig(ctx context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {\n\tcmd, _ := clabexec.NewExecCmdFromString(saveCmd)\n\n\texecResult, err := n.RunExec(ctx, cmd)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"%s: failed to execute cmd: %v\", n.Cfg.ShortName, err)\n\t}\n\n\tif execResult.GetStdErrString() != \"\" {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"%s errors: %s\", n.Cfg.ShortName, execResult.GetStdErrString())\n\t}\n\n\tcfgPath := filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, \"config\", \"config.json\")\n\n\tlog.Infof(\n\t\t\"saved SR Linux configuration from %s node. {\n\t\t\t\tlog.Debugf(\n\t\t\t\t\t\"error during checking SR Linux boot status: %s\",\n\t\t\t\t\texecResult.GetStdErrString(),\n\t\t\t\t)\n\t\t\t\ttime.Sleep(retryTimer)\n\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tif !strings.Contains(execResult.GetStdOutString(), \"running\") {\n\t\t\t\ttime.Sleep(retryTimer)\n\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\t// once mgmt server is running, we need to check if it is ready to accept configuration\n\t\t\t// commands\n\t\t\t// this is step-start
{
  "type": "step-start",
  "id": "prt_1b276a991001vIt7aomiwuo0M7",
  "sessionID": "ses_e4d89a146ffe8jfMgtpr7trDyT",

Reasoning
Now let's understand the change's context. The PR introduces `deployedWithoutNetnsNode` to handle the case where a node was deployed by an older containerlab (before the netns holder feature) and is being started/GetNSPath'd by a new containerlab: the holder container doesn't exist, but the node container does. In that case, fall back to the node's own netns.

Key concerns:

1. `deployedWithoutNetnsNode` is a heuristic: holder NotFound AND node not NotFound. But what if the node container is in `Stopping`/`Paused`/whatever state? Still not NotFound, so falls to node path — fine. What if the holder exists but the node is NotFound (lab partially destroyed)? Then deployedWithoutNetnsNode=false, so `GetNSPath` calls netnsNode.GetNSPath — same as before. Fine.

2. But consider `Start`: with the fallback, the node was deployed without a holder, and Start calls `n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx)`. DefaultNode.Start presumably starts the container with n.Cfg as-is (with port bindings, mgmt net, etc. — i.e., a pre-holder deploy config). That's fine for old deployments. But wait — the test `TestStartOfNodeDeployedWithoutNetnsNode` mocks GetContainerStatus(node)=Stopped and StartContainer. But note that Start is called with node Stopped and holder NotFound; after Start, the node now runs with its own netns. Subsequent `GetNSPath` works via fallback. Good.

3. Inconsistency: `Stop` — when netnsNode != nil, Stop stops the holder. For a node deployed without holder, the holder isn't running, so Stop returns nil early (`GetContainerStatus != Running → return nil`). Fine.

4. `Delete`: deletes both; netnsNode.Delete on NotFound container — presumably DeleteContainer on nonexistent returns nil or error? Let me check NetnsHolder.Delete. Actually it wraps `n.DefaultNode.Delete` and `netnsNode.Delete`. The DefaultNode.Delete probably handles NotFound gracefully. Let's check.

5. `GetContainers`: for a node deployed without holder — holder NotFound → holder.GetContainers returns ErrContainersNotFound → returns cnts (the node's own container). But the node container (pre-holder deploy) has real network settings, ports, etc. since it was created with its own network. DefaultNode.GetContainers returns those. Good.

6. `PostDeploy`, `SaveConfig` — unchanged.

7. `Deploy`: when netnsNode != nil, Deploy creates the holder. If the lab already has a node deployed without holder and the user runs `clab deploy` again (idempotent redeploy) — what happens? The node already exists (created by old clab without holder). New clab's Deploy would attempt to create a new container with NetworkMode container:holder — but the node container already exists → CreateContainer fails with "already exists"? How does Deploy handle existing containers? Let me check how containerlab handles deploy when containers already exist — the runtime `CreateContainer` may return an error if exists. Actually there's a `--create`/redeploy flow: `clab deploy` on an existing lab typically errors or the node is stopped first? Hmm. Actually in containerlab, `deploy` after `destroy` recreates. Running `deploy` when containers exist... Let me check `CheckDeploymentConditions` / how nodes handle existing containers. There's `n.Runtime.ContainerExists`? Let me check DefaultNode.Deploy.

Actually more importantly, the fix means: legacy deployed node remains working until "it is recreated" (per comment). But if the user runs `clab deploy` again on the same lab with the new clab version, what happens? The netns holder gets deployed (new container), then `n.Runtime.CreateContainer(ctx, n.containerConfig())` fails because container already exists → deploy error telling user to destroy first. That's presumably the accepted behavior per the comment "until it is recreated". Let me check if there's an error path/known pattern.

Let me check DefaultNode.Deploy and how existing containers are handled, plus runtime.GetContainerStatus semantics and NetnsHolder methods.

8. `GetNSPath` now performs two runtime status calls on every invocation (holder status + node status). GetNSPath is called per endpoint during deploy/postdeploy and possibly by tools (veth create, disable-tx-offload, exec?). Frequency is modest; per-call two status lookups. Prior behavior: one GetNSPath call on holder. Now in fallback case: GetContainerStatus(holder) + GetContainerStatus(node) + DefaultNode.GetNSPath. Not a perf problem. But there's a subtle issue: `GetNSPath` calls `n.GetContainerStatus(ctx)` — does DefaultNode's GetNSPath use the node's own ns path? Let me check.

Wait — actually there's a potential bug: `deployedWithoutNetnsNode` calls `n.GetContainerStatus(ctx)` — the srl node's GetContainerStatus, which is inherited from DefaultNode? Let me check whether srl overrides GetContainerStatus. The grep found only 9 matches; GetContainerStatus not among them, so it uses the embedded DefaultNode. DefaultNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx) → runtime.GetContainerStatus(ctx, n.Cfg.LongName)? Let me verify.

9. Race/consistency: In `Start`, `deployedWithoutNetnsNode` is checked; if holder is NotFound and node is Stopped, use DefaultNode.Start. Good. But then after the node starts, the holder still doesn't exist; `linkNetns` isn't called (good, since netns symlink would point to node itself... actually wait — DefaultNode.Start doesn't call linkNetns; but for a legacy node, the netns symlink may already exist pointing to the node's ns path from old clab. Fine.)

But: what about the case where the holder NotFound and node Running, and `Start` is called — DefaultNode.Start on a running container? StartContainer on running container — runtime may error ("container already started")? The old behavior: netnsNode.EnsureRunning first, which returns error "recreate the node"? Actually the base Start: if netnsNode != nil, EnsureRunning(ctx, shortName) — this checks holder exists; if NotFound it errors with "recreate the node"? Let me check NetnsHolder.EnsureRunning. In base, TestStartRequiresNetnsNode expects error containing "recreate the node" when holder is NotFound. So the new change makes Start succeed in that scenario when the node container itself exists (legacy). But if node also NotFound → error remains. The test was updated with AnyTimes for both.

10. Potential issue: `deployedWithoutNetnsNode` semantics vs a mid-state: holder NotFound, node not NotFound but node is being deployed fresh? During fresh deploy, Deploy is called before Start; the holder exists then. Not an issue.

11. What about `Stop` for legacy node: DefaultNode.Stop, then `n.netnsNode.GetContainerStatus != Running → return nil`. Fine.

12. Now the bigger consistency question: other places that branch on `n.netnsNode == nil` — `Deploy`, `Delete`, `GetContainers`, `DeleteNetnsSymlink`, and wherever `containerConfig`/`linkNetns` are used. For a legacy deployed node (netnsNode != nil but holder absent), these still use holder semantics. Notably `GetContainers` handles holder-missing gracefully. `Delete` deletes both — DeleteContainer on missing holder presumably fine.

But what about `PostDeploy`? It calls RunExec on the node container — fine.

What about `SaveConfig`? fine.

What about netns symlink created during fresh deploy by new clab — `linkNetns` in Deploy. For legacy, not relevant.

What about `CheckDeploymentConditions`? Calls DefaultNode. Fine.

What about tools like `clab tools veth create` / `disable-tx-offload` that use GetNSPath? They now get the correct ns path for legacy nodes. Good — that's the point of the fix.

13. Now check: does the diff also need updating for `Deploy` path when legacy node exists? Consider user with legacy deployment runs `clab deploy` again (without destroy). Containerlab's deploy flow: for each node, Deploy is called. Before that, is there a check whether containers already exist? Let me check the runtime/deploy flow. In containerlab, `clab deploy` on an existing lab: nodes' Deploy → CreateContainer → error "container already exists"? Actually I recall containerlab handles this via `redeploy` or errors out. Hmm, there's a check in cmd deploy: if lab is already deployed, it... Let me check. Actually containerlab historically just errors when creating a container that exists? No — I believe `clab deploy` on an existing lab errors with "container already exists"? Actually no: newer versions support `clab deploy` being idempotent? Let me check the code.

Let me search for how the holder comment "until it is recreated" is handled — maybe there's a release note. Also check `docs/rn/0.83.md` maybe contains notes. But docs aren't in the changed list, so this PR didn't update docs — is this user-visible behavior? It's a bugfix for legacy deployments; docs might mention netns holder. Check docs/manual/kinds/srl.md for netns-holder mention and whether it needs a note about legacy nodes. That's a P4-ish doc finding possibly.

Let me look at NetnsHolder implementation: nodes/netns_holder? Let's find.

11: // NetnsHolder is an internal container that owns the network namespace of a node. The node's
12: // containers join it, so the links and the management address outlive their restarts.
13: type NetnsHolder struct {
14: 	DefaultNode
15: }
16: 
17: func (n *NetnsHolder) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...NodeOption) error {
18: 	n.DefaultNode = *NewDefaultNode(n)
19: 	n.Cfg = cfg
20: 	n.StopSignal = clabtypes.SIGTERM
21: 
22: 	for _, o := range opts {
23: 		o(n)
24: 	}
25: 
26: 	return nil
27: }
28: 
29: // EnsureRunning starts or unpauses the holder, so that the containers of node can join it.
30: func (n *NetnsHolder) EnsureRunning(ctx context.Context, node string) error {
31: 	switch status := n.GetContainerStatus(ctx); status {
32: 	case clabruntime.Running:
33: 	case clabruntime.Created, clabruntime.Stopped:
34: 		if err := n.Start(ctx); err != nil {
35: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q network namespace container start error: %w", node, err)
36: 		}
37: 	case clabruntime.Paused:
38: 		if err := n.Runtime.UnpauseContainer(ctx, n.Cfg.LongName); err != nil {
39: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q network namespace container unpause error: %w", node, err)
40: 		}
41: 	case clabruntime.NotFound:
42: 		return fmt.Errorf(
43: 			"node %q network namespace container %q not found; recreate the node",
44: 			node, n.Cfg.LongName,
45: 		)
46: 	default:
47: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q network namespace container %q is %s",

51: 	return nil

74: func (n *sros) cleanupParkingNetNS() error {
75: 	name := clabutils.ParkingNetnsName(n.Cfg.LongName)
76: 	if _, err := clabutils.GetNamedNetNS(name); err != nil {
77: 		return nil
78: 	}
79:

81: }

83: // GetNSPath retrieves the Namespace Path.
84: func (n *sros) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
85: 	if n.isStandaloneNode() || (n.isDistributedCardNode() && n.rootCtrName == "") {
86: 		return n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
87: 	} else if n.isDistributedCardNode() {
88: 		return n.Runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, n.rootCtrName)
89: 	}
90: 	return n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
91: }
92: 
93: // DeleteNetnsSymlink deletes the symlink file created for the container netns.
94: func (n *sros) DeleteNetnsSymlink() error {
95: 	var errs []error
96: 
97: 	// if it is the base node, then we need to delete the symlink for all the components.
98: 	if n.isDistributedBaseNode() {
99: 		for _, componentNode := range n.componentNodes {
100: 			errs = append(errs, componentNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
101: 		}
102: 		if n.netnsNode != nil {
103: 			errs = append(errs, n.netnsNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
104: 		}
105: 		errs = append(errs, clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(n.Cfg.LongName))
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	errs = append(errs, n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink(), n.cleanupParkingNetNS())
109: 
110: 	return errors.Join(errs...)

Reasoning
Now, key question: does the same legacy-deployment issue exist for `sros`? sros has the same netns holder machinery (`EnsureRunning` in sros.go:1827). The PR only fixes srl. If sros nodes deployed pre-holder hit the same issue (Start fails with "recreate the node", GetNSPath fails), the fix is incomplete. Let me check sros.Start and GetNSPath — sros GetNSPath has no deployedWithoutNetnsNode fallback; if netnsNode != nil (which is always true for non-standalone sros?), it would call netnsNode.GetNSPath → error. Let me check sros.go Start.

Also need to check: is `netnsNode` always non-nil for srl when usesNetnsNode()? In initNetnsNode it's only set when usesNetnsNode. So `n.netnsNode == nil` means host-network mode etc.

Important check: in `deployedWithoutNetnsNode`, when netnsNode is nil it would panic — but both callers guard `n.netnsNode == nil ||` before calling it (short-circuit). Only two call sites. OK.

Another check: `n.GetContainerStatus(ctx)` — DefaultNode.GetContainerStatus. Let me verify it exists in default_node.go and what name it uses. Also check runtime status enum values: NotFound, Running, Stopped, Created, Paused.

Edge: what if the node container is NotFound but holder NotFound too (destroyed lab) → Start: netnsNode != nil, deployedWithoutNetnsNode=false → EnsureRunning → error "recreate the node". Good (test covers).

Edge: holder NotFound, node Paused/Created? e.g., node container exists but paused. deployedWithoutNetnsNode = true (status != NotFound) → DefaultNode.Start → StartContainer on paused container? Runtime StartContainer on a paused container errors? Hmm, for docker, starting an already-created container is fine; paused container start → error "cannot start a paused container"? Actually docker: `docker start` on paused container returns an error? I believe it errors ("cannot start a paused container, try unpause"). Edge case, minor.

More important: what about the sros kind having the same gap? That's a real finding candidate: incomplete fix — the same legacy-deployment scenario applies to sros which uses the identical holder mechanism, but sros.Start/GetNSPath lack the fallback. Let me check sros.go Start around 1827.

  Line 90: For each target, containerlab enters the node's network namespace, opens a Linux packet socket (`AF_PACKET`) bound to the interface, and leaves the namespace again; the socket keeps capturing on that interface. This is what `tcpdump` does inside the node, without needing `tcpdump` in the node or on the host. All sockets are opened before the first byte is written, so a capture that cannot start (a node that does not run, a missing interface) fails without output.

Reasoning
The 0.83 release notes at docs/rn/0.83.md:142 describe the holder. The PR adds legacy compatibility without a release note update. Is 0.83 the unreleased/current release notes? It mentions #3577 already. This change might warrant a release note entry. Minor doc finding (P4), given the release-notes-review rule exists in the repo (.cursor/rules/release-note-review.mdc). Let me check that rule file quickly.

Now let me think about deeper correctness issues.

Issue candidate 1: `deployedWithoutNetnsNode` treats holder NotFound + node not-NotFound as "legacy". But consider a *new* clab deploying a fresh node: Deploy creates holder first, then node. If the user interrupts deploy after holder creation but... holder exists then. Fine.

But consider `clab destroy` partially done: node deleted, holder still exists → GetNSPath → holder path; fine.

Consider holder deleted but node running (e.g., user manually removed the holder container, or the holder crashed and got removed by restart policy "no"? Holder has restart policy unless-stopped by default). Then GetNSPath → falls back to node's own ns path — which is now actually correct behavior since the node container has its own netns? Wait, no: with the holder model, the node container is created with `NetworkMode: container:holder`, so it shares the holder's netns. If the holder is gone (removed), the node container would... when the holder container is removed, the shared netns persists as long as at least one container uses it? In Docker, when the "parent" container (referenced by container: mode) is removed, containers joined to its netns keep the netns alive (netns reference counted at the kernel level; Docker handles this — actually if you remove the holder while the node is running, the node keeps its netns until it restarts). The node's own netns path (/proc/<pid>/ns/net of the node container) is the same netns as the holder's. So fallback via `n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath` → `Runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, node name)` — resolves the node container's own netns, which is the same netns. So it's correct in that scenario too.

Actually wait — is it? `Runtime.GetNSPath` for docker resolves via the container's PID? Let me check docker runtime GetNSPath.

Issue candidate 2: `Start` for legacy node calls `n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx)`. Let me check what DefaultNode.Start does — it might call PostDeployEndpoints, linkNetns? Let me read DefaultNode.Start fully.

Issue candidate 3: In `GetNSPath`, the fallback happens when holder is NotFound. But `GetNSPath` is used during fresh deploy of a *new* clab: Deploy → CreateContainer(node, container:holder), StartContainer, then `linkNetns` which calls `n.netnsNode.GetNSPath` directly (not n.GetNSPath), so no issue.

But what about `PostDeploy` and endpoint/link operations after deploy that call `n.GetNSPath`? During normal deploy, holder exists → fine.

Issue candidate 4: Consider the race where GetContainerStatus of the holder errors vs returns NotFound. GetContainerStatus returns NotFound when container doesn't exist; errors propagate? Let me check docker runtime GetContainerStatus — it likely returns NotFound on inspect error with no such container, and possibly NotFound for other errors. Let me check.

Issue candidate 5: `Start` legacy path — after DefaultNode.Start, the netns symlink: for legacy deployments, old clab created the netns symlink pointing to the node container (the node's own ns path at time of deploy). If the node container restarts, its PID changes and the symlink becomes stale. Old clab's Start — did it relink? In old clab (before holder), was there a linkNetns? Let's check base srl.go: was there a linkNetns call in base? The base netns.go already has linkNetns (used in base srl.go Start? let me check). Actually base diff only changed `netnsNode == nil ||` in Start, so base Start also called linkNetns after DefaultNode.Start when netnsNode != nil. Base behavior for legacy: Start fails at EnsureRunning with "recreate the node". New behavior: legacy node starts via DefaultNode.Start and... hmm wait — new Start: if deployedWithoutNetnsNode → `return n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx)` — no linkNetns call. The comment says the legacy node keeps working until it is recreated. But does the legacy node need the netns symlink refreshed on start? The symlink at /var/run/netns/<LongName> created by old clab points to the node's old netns path (by PID). After host reboot (all containers gone), the user runs `clab deploy` or `start`? Actually after reboot, containers would be gone entirely or restarted by restart policy. For legacy nodes started via restart policy after reboot, the symlink would be stale. Old clab Start (pre-holder era) — did it call LinkContainerNS on start? Let me check git history... can't. Let me check what base's DefaultNode.Start does regarding netns linking, and what utils.LinkContainerNS does — maybe it resolves the current path each time, in which case staleness matters only for consumers of the symlink file.

Let me look at utils/netlink.go LinkContainerNS.

1941: // GetContainerStatus retrieves the ContainerStatus of the named container.
1942: func (d *DockerRuntime) GetContainerStatus(
1943: 	ctx context.Context,
1944: 	cID string,
1945: ) clabruntime.ContainerStatus {
1946: 	inspect, err := d.Client.ContainerInspect(ctx, cID)
1947: 	if err != nil {
1948: 		return clabruntime.NotFound
1949: 	}
1950: 	switch inspect.State.Status {
1951: 	case "running":

1954: 		return clabruntime.Paused

1958: 		return clabruntime.Restarting

Reasoning
Note: `GetContainerStatus` swallows all inspect errors into `NotFound`. So `deployedWithoutNetnsNode` can also misfire if the Docker daemon is unreachable/temporarily erroring on inspect — then holder status = NotFound (error swallowed) and if the node inspect succeeds (or also fails → NotFound, which makes the second condition false). Actually if both inspects fail, both return NotFound → deployedWithoutNetnsNode = false → normal path → `netnsNode.GetNSPath` → Runtime.GetNSPath → inspect error → returns error. So degraded daemon produces errors, not silent wrong behavior. If only the holder inspect fails (transient) and node is Running → fallback to node's ns path, which for a holder-model deployment is... the node container joined the holder's netns, so node's /proc/<pid>/ns/net is the same netns object. So the path differs but points to the same namespace. Fine.

Now the bigger scenario check: legacy node + `clab deploy` again. Let me look at how deploy handles existing lab: cmd/root or lab.go "isdeployed" checks. Search for "Deployed" state checks in lab.go — e.g., `lab.CheckDeploymentConditions` or skip if deployed. In containerlab, `clab deploy` on an already-deployed lab: nodes' Deploy is called for all nodes; CreateContainer with existing name → docker returns "Conflict. The container name ... is already in use"? Actually I recall containerlab handles that: runtime CreateContainer checks if container exists and removes it? Let me check docker CreateContainer.

Not directly relevant. Let me now focus on the key remaining checks:

1. Does `Deploy` for the srl node handle the "legacy node exists" scenario? If the user runs `clab deploy` on a legacy-deployed lab with the new binary: netnsNode != nil → holder Deploy (creates holder container) → then `CreateContainer(node containerConfig with container:holder netmode)` → docker: container name already in use → error? Let me check whether deploy pre-checks existing containers. Search for how containerlab handles re-deploy when containers exist — maybe in `lab.go` there's a check that errors "lab is already deployed".

30: // DeployResult holds the outcome of a Deploy call.
31: type DeployResult struct {
32: 	Containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
33: 	// Apply summarizes the reconciliation of an already deployed lab or the dry-run plan;
34: 	// it is nil when Deploy performed a fresh full deployment.

40: // Deploy converges the lab to the requested topology. A lab without runtime state is
41: // deployed from scratch, an already deployed lab is reconciled in place.
42: func (c *CLab) Deploy(
43: 	ctx context.Context,
44: 	options *DeployOptions,
45: ) (*DeployResult, error) {
46: 	if options == nil {
47: 		var err error
48: 		options, err = NewDeployOptions(0)
49: 		if err != nil {
50: 			return nil, err
51: 		}
52: 	}
53: 
54: 	if options.reconfigure {
55: 		if options.dryRun {
56: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
57: 				"dry-run cannot be combined with reconfigure: " +
58: 					"reconfigure always destroys and redeploys the full lab",
59: 			)
60: 		}
61: 
62: 		containers, err := c.deploy(ctx, options)
63: 		if err != nil {
64: 			return nil, err
65: 		}
66: 
67: 		return &DeployResult{Containers: containers}, nil
68: 	}
69: 
70: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil && options.dryRun {
71: 		planner, ok := c.LabRuntime.(clablabruntime.TopologyPlanner)
72: 		if !ok {
73: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("lab runtime %q does not support dry-run", c.globalRuntimeName)
74: 		}
75: 
76: 		req, err := c.labRuntimeDeployRequest()
77: 		if err != nil {
78: 			return nil, err
79: 		}
80: 		applyLabRuntimeDeployOptions(&req, options)
81: 
82: 		plan, err := planner.Plan(ctx, req)
83: 		if err != nil {
84: 			return nil, err
85: 		}
86: 
87: 		return &DeployResult{RuntimePlan: plan}, nil
88: 	}
89: 
90: 	currentNodes, err := c.runtimeNodeGroups(ctx)
91: 	if err != nil {
92: 		return nil, err
93: 	}
94: 	initialDeploy, err := c.needsInitialDeploy(currentNodes)
95: 	if err != nil {
96: 		return nil, err
97: 	}
98: 	if initialDeploy {
99: 		if options.dryRun {
100: 			return &DeployResult{
101: 				Apply: &ApplyResult{
102: 					DryRun:      true,
103: 					DeployedLab: true,
104: 					LabName:     c.Config.Name,
105: 				},
106: 			}, nil
107: 		}
108: 
109: 		containers, err := c.deploy(ctx, options)
110: 		if err != nil {
111: 			return nil, err
112: 		}
113: 
114: 		return &DeployResult{Containers: containers}, nil
115: 	}
116: 
117: 	if err := c.checkReconcileDeployOptions(options); err != nil {
118: 		return nil, err
119: 	}
120: 
121: 	applyOptions := &ApplyOptions{
122: 		dryRun:             options.dryRun,
123: 		skipPostDeploy:     options.skipPostDeploy,
124: 		skipLabDirFileACLs: options.skipLabDirFileACLs,
125: 		graph:              options.graph,
126: 		finalizeNoop:       true,
127: 		maxWorkers:         options.maxWorkers,
128: 		exportTemplate:     options.exportTemplate,
129: 	}
130: 	applyResult, err := c.apply(ctx, applyOptions, currentNodes)
131: 	if err != nil {
132: 		return nil, err
133: 	}
134: 
135: 	if options.dryRun {
136: 		return &DeployResult{Apply: applyResult}, nil
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	containers, err := c.ListNodesContainers(ctx)
140: 	if err != nil {
141: 		return nil, err
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	return &DeployResult{Containers: containers, Apply: applyResult}, nil
145: }
146: 
147: // checkReconcileDeployOptions rejects deploy options that only make sense for a fresh
148: // deployment when deploy is about to reconcile an already deployed lab.
149: func (c *CLab) checkReconcileDeployOptions(options *DeployOptions) error {
150: 	if c.managementNetworkOverridden {
151: 		return fmt.Errorf(
152: 			"management network overrides require a fresh deployment, but lab %q is already "+
153: 				"deployed; use --reconfigure to destroy and redeploy it",
154: 			c.Config.Name,
155: 		)
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	if options.restoreAll != "" || len(options.restoreNodeSnapshots) > 0 {
159: 		return fmt.Errorf(
160: 			"snapshot restore requires a fresh deployment, but lab %q is already deployed; "+
161: 				"use --reconfigure to destroy and redeploy it",
162: 			c.Config.Name,
163: 		)
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	if len(c.nodeFilter) > 0 {
167: 		return fmt.Errorf(
168: 			"node filter is not supported when reconciling deployed lab %q: "+
169: 				"running nodes excluded by the filter would be deleted; "+
170: 				"remove the filter or use --reconfigure",
171: 			c.Config.Name,
172: 		)
173: 	}
174: 
175: 	return nil
176: }

178: // deploy creates a lab that has no managed runtime nodes yet.
179: // skipcq: GO-R1005
180: func (c *CLab) deploy( //nolint: funlen
181: 	ctx context.Context,
182: 	options *DeployOptions,
183: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
184: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
185: 		return c.deployWithLabRuntime(ctx, options)
186: 	}
187: 
188: 	var err error
189: 
190: 	err = c.ResolveLinks()
191: 	if err != nil {
192: 		return nil, err
193: 	}
194: 
195: 	log.Debugf("lab Conf: %+v", c.Config)
196: 
197: 	if options.reconfigure {
198: 		_ = c.destroy(ctx, uint(len(c.Nodes)), true)
199: 		log.Info("Removing directory", "path", c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir())
200: 
201: 		if err := os.RemoveAll(c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()); err != nil {
202: 			return nil, err
203: 		}
204: 	}
205: 
206: 	err = c.prepareLabManagementNetwork(ctx)
207: 	if err != nil {
208: 		return nil, err
209: 	}
210: 
211: 	if err := c.checkTopologyDefinition(ctx); err != nil {
212: 		return nil, err
213: 	}
214: 
215: 	if err := c.prepareDeployArtifacts(ctx, options.skipLabDirFileACLs); err != nil {
216: 		return nil, err
217: 	}
218: 
219: 	// Apply snapshot restore configuration to nodes
220: 	if err := c.configureSnapshotRestore(options); err != nil {
221: 		return nil, err
222: 	}
223: 
224: 	nodesWg, execCollection, nodeFailCh, err := c.createNodes(
225: 		ctx,
226: 		options.maxWorkers,
227: 		options.skipPostDeploy,
228: 	)
229: 	if err != nil {
230: 		return nil, err
231: 	}
232: 
233: 	if err := waitForNodeDeploy(ctx, nodesWg, nodeFailCh); err != nil {
234: 		return nil, err
235: 	}
236: 	if err := c.syncTailscaleProxy(ctx); err != nil {
237: 		return nil, err
238: 	}
239: 	if err := c.SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx); err != nil {
240: 		return nil, err
241: 	}
242: 
243: 	// also call deploy on the special nodes endpoints (only host is required for the
244: 	// vxlan stitched endpoints).
245: 	// this must happen after all node workers have finished so that veth pairs created
246: 	// as part of vxlan-stitch links are already deployed before the stitch TC rules are applied.
247: 	eps := c.getSpecialLinkNodes()["host"].GetEndpoints()
248: 	for _, ep := range eps {
249: 		err = ep.Deploy(ctx)
250: 		if err != nil {
251: 			log.Warnf("failed deploying endpoint %s", ep)
252: 		}
253: 	}
254: 
255: 	// Stitch links after node workers and host endpoints have finished.
256: 	// The veth pair is already created by node workers and the VxLAN interface is created
257: 	// by host endpoint deploy; Stitch applies the TC redirect rules to bridge them.
258: 	var linkPostDeployWorkers errgroup.Group
259: 	linkPostDeployWorkers.SetLimit(int(options.maxWorkers))
260: 	for _, link := range c.Links {
261: 		linkPostDeployWorkers.Go(func() error {
262: 			if err := link.PostDeploy(ctx); err != nil {
263: 				log.Warnf("failed post-deploying link: %v", err)
264: 			}
265: 			return nil
266: 		})
267: 	}
268: 	_ = linkPostDeployWorkers.Wait()
269: 
270: 	execCollection.Log()
271: 
272: 	return c.finalize(ctx, options.exportTemplate, options.graph)
273: }
274: 
275: func waitForNodeDeploy(
276: 	ctx context.Context,
277: 	nodesWg *sync.WaitGroup,
278: 	nodeFailCh chan error,
279: ) error {
280: 	if nodesWg != nil {
281: 		nodesWg.Wait()
282: 	}
283: 
284: 	close(nodeFailCh)
285: 
286: 	// Cancellation takes precedence over errors caused by workers unwinding, and
287: 	// prevents post-deploy work from running against a partial lab.
288: 	if err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {
289: 		return err
290: 	}
291: 
292: 	var nodeFailErrs []error
293: 	for nodeErr := range nodeFailCh {
294: 		nodeFailErrs = append(nodeFailErrs, nodeErr)
295: 	}
296: 	if len(nodeFailErrs) > 0 {
297: 		return fmt.Errorf(
298: 			"deployment failed for one or more nodes: %w",
299: 			errors.Join(nodeFailErrs...),
300: 		)
301: 	}
302: 
303: 	return nil
304: }
305: 
306: // prepareLabManagementNetwork creates the management networks that are in use
307: // and allocates addresses for the nodes attached to them.
308: func (c *CLab) prepareLabManagementNetwork(
309: 	ctx context.Context,
310: 	existing ...clabtypes.ExistingAddress,
311: ) error {
312: 	if err := c.CreateNetwork(ctx); err != nil {
313: 		return err
314: 	}
315: 
316: 	if err := c.allocateLabManagementIPs(ctx, existing); err != nil {
317: 		return err
318: 	}
319: 
320: 	return c.setMgmtNetLinkBridges()
321: }
322: 
323: func (c *CLab) allocateLabManagementIPs(
324: 	ctx context.Context,
325: 	existing []clabtypes.ExistingAddress,
326: ) error {
327: 	preferred := make(map[string]clabtypes.NodeAddresses)
328: 	state, err := c.LoadState()
329: 	if err != nil {
330: 		log.Warn("Ignoring preferred management addresses", "error", err)
331: 	}
332: 
333: 	if state != nil {
334: 		for name, addresses := range state.IPAM {
335: 			preferred[name] = addresses
336: 		}
337: 	}
338: 
339: 	for _, m := range c.allMgmtNetworks() {
340: 		if c.skipMgmtNetwork(m) {
341: 			continue
342: 		}
343: 		nodes := c.mgmtNetNodes(m)
344: 		if m.IPAM.Provider == clabtypes.IPAMProviderRuntime || len(nodes) == 0 {
345: 			continue
346: 		}
347: 
348: 		reserved, err := c.collectReservedManagementAddresses(ctx, m, existing)
349: 		if err != nil {
350: 			return err
351: 		}
352: 
353: 		err = mgmt.AllocateManagementIPs(
354: 			ctx,
355: 			m,
356: 			nodes,
357: 			clabtypes.AllocationOptions{
358: 				Existing:  existing,
359: 				Preferred: preferred,
360: 				Reserved:  reserved,
361: 			},
362: 		)
363: 		if err != nil {
364: 			return err
365: 		}
366: 	}
367: 
368: 	return nil
369: }
370: 
371: func (c *CLab) collectReservedManagementAddresses(
372: 	ctx context.Context,
373: 	m *clabtypes.MgmtNet,
374: 	existing []clabtypes.ExistingAddress,
375: ) ([]netip.Addr, error) {
376: 	var subnets []netip.Prefix
377: 	for _, value := range []string{m.IPv4Subnet, m.IPv6Subnet} {
378: 		if value == "" {
379: 			continue
380: 		}
381: 		subnet, err := netip.ParsePrefix(value)
382: 		if err != nil {
383: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("management subnet %q: %w", value, err)
384: 		}
385: 		subnets = append(subnets, subnet)
386: 	}
387: 	occupied, err := c.globalRuntime().NetworkAddresses(ctx, subnets)
388: 	if err != nil {
389: 		return nil, err
390: 	}
391: 	type ownerAddress struct {
392: 		containerID string
393: 		address     netip.Addr
394: 	}
395: 	reused := make(map[ownerAddress]bool, len(existing))
396: 	for _, entry := range existing {
397: 		if entry.ContainerID != "" {
398: 			reused[ownerAddress{entry.ContainerID, entry.Address}] = true
399: 		}
400: 	}
401: 
402: 	var reserved []netip.Addr
403: 	for _, entry := range occupied {
404: 		if entry.NetworkName == m.Network &&
405: 			reused[ownerAddress{entry.ContainerID, entry.Address}] {
406: 			continue
407: 		}
408: 		reserved = append(reserved, entry.Address)
409: 	}
410: 	return reserved, nil
411: }
412: 
413: func (c *CLab) prepareDeployArtifacts(ctx context.Context, skipLabDirFileACLs bool) error {
414: 	if err := c.loadKernelModules(); err != nil {
415: 		return err
416: 	}
417: 
418: 	c.prepareLabDirectory(skipLabDirFileACLs)
419: 
420: 	if err := c.createPlaceholderArtifacts(); err != nil {
421: 		return err
422: 	}
423: 
424: 	if err := c.setupNodeAuth(ctx); err != nil {
425: 		return err
426: 	}
427: 
428: 	c.populateExtraHosts()
429: 
430: 	return nil
431: }

433: func (c *CLab) prepareLabDirectory(skipFileACLs bool) {
434: 	log.Info("Creating lab directory", "path", c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir())
435: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir(), clabconstants.PermissionsDirDefault)
436: 
437: 	if skipFileACLs {
438: 		return
439: 	}
440: 
441: 	if err := clabutils.AdjustFileACLs(c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()); err != nil {
442: 		log.Infof("unable to adjust Labdir file ACLs: %v", err)
443: 	}
444: }

446: func (c *CLab) createPlaceholderArtifacts() error {
447: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(
448: 		c.TopoPaths.CABaseDir(),
449: 		clabconstants.PermissionsOpen,
450: 	)
451: 
452: 	paths := []string{
453: 		c.TopoPaths.AnsibleInventoryFileAbsPath(),
454: 		c.TopoPaths.NornirSimpleInventoryFileAbsPath(),
455: 		c.TopoPaths.TopoExportFile(),
456: 	}
457: 
458: 	for _, path := range paths {
459: 		if err := createEmptyFile(path); err != nil {
460: 			return err
461: 		}
462: 	}
463: 
464: 	return nil
465: }
466: 
467: func createEmptyFile(path string) error {
468: 	f, err := os.Create(path)
469: 	if err != nil {
470: 		return err
471: 	}
472: 
473: 	return f.Close()
474: }

476: func (c *CLab) setupNodeAuth(ctx context.Context) error {
477: 	if err := c.certificateAuthoritySetup(); err != nil {
478: 		return err
479: 	}
480: 
481: 	var err error
482: 	c.SSHPubKeys, err = c.RetrieveSSHPubKeys(ctx)
483: 	if err != nil {
484: 		log.Warn(err)
485: 	}
486: 
487: 	return c.createAuthzKeysFile()
488: }
489: 
490: func (c *CLab) populateExtraHosts() {
491: 	extraHosts := make([]string, 0, len(c.Nodes))
492: 
493: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
494: 		n := c.Nodes[nodeName]
495: 		if n.Config().MgmtIPv4Address != "" {
496: 			log.Debugf("Adding static ipv4 /etc/hosts entry for %s:%s",
497: 				n.Config().ShortName, n.Config().MgmtIPv4Address)
498: 			extraHosts = append(extraHosts, n.Config().ShortName+":"+n.Config().MgmtIPv4Address)
499: 		}
500: 		if n.Config().MgmtIPv6Address != "" {
501: 			log.Debugf("Adding static ipv6 /etc/hosts entry for %s:%s",
502: 				n.Config().ShortName, n.Config().MgmtIPv6Address)
503: 			extraHosts = append(extraHosts, n.Config().ShortName+":"+n.Config().MgmtIPv6Address)
504: 		}
505: 	}
506: 
507: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
508: 		n := c.Nodes[nodeName]
509: 		if !strings.HasPrefix(n.Config().NetworkMode, "container:") {
510: 			n.Config().ExtraHosts = extraHosts
511: 		}
512: 	}
513: }
514: 
515: // certificateAuthoritySetup sets up the certificate authority parameters.
516: func (c *CLab) certificateAuthoritySetup() error {
517: 	// init the Cert storage and CA
518: 	c.Cert.CertStorage = clabcert.NewLocalDirCertStorage(c.TopoPaths)
519: 	c.Cert.CA = clabcert.NewCA()
520: 
521: 	s := c.Config.Settings
522: 
523: 	// Set defaults for the CA parameters
524: 	keySize := 2048
525: 	validityDuration := time.Until(time.Now().AddDate(1, 0, 0)) // 1 year as default
526: 	var extCACert, extCAKey string
527: 
528: 	// check that Settings.CertificateAuthority exists.
529: 	if s != nil && s.CertificateAuthority != nil {
530: 		// if ValidityDuration is set use the value
531: 		if s.CertificateAuthority.ValidityDuration != 0 {
532: 			validityDuration = s.CertificateAuthority.ValidityDuration
533: 		}
534: 
535: 		// if KeyLength is set use the value
536: 		if s.CertificateAuthority.KeySize != 0 {
537: 			keySize = s.CertificateAuthority.KeySize
538: 		}
539: 
540: 		// if external CA cert and key are set, propagate to topopaths
541: 		extCACert = s.CertificateAuthority.Cert
542: 		extCAKey = s.CertificateAuthority.Key
543: 	}
544: 
545: 	// override external ca and key from env vars
546: 	if v := os.Getenv("CLAB_CA_KEY_FILE"); v != "" {
547: 		extCAKey = v
548: 	}
549: 
550: 	if v := os.Getenv("CLAB_CA_CERT_FILE"); v != "" {
551: 		extCACert = v
552: 	}
553: 
554: 	if extCACert != "" && extCAKey != "" {
555: 		err := c.TopoPaths.SetExternalCaFiles(extCACert, extCAKey)
556: 		if err != nil {
557: 			return err
558: 		}
559: 	}
560: 
561: 	// define the attributes used to generate the CA Cert
562: 	caCertInput := &clabcert.CACSRInput{
563: 		CommonName:   c.Config.Name + " lab CA",
564: 		Country:      "US",
565: 		Expiry:       validityDuration,
566: 		Organization: "containerlab",
567: 		KeySize:      keySize,
568: 	}
569: 
570: 	return c.LoadOrGenerateCA(caCertInput)
571: }
572: 
573: // createNodes schedules nodes creation and returns a waitgroup for all nodes
574: // with the exec collection created from the exec config of each node.
575: // The exec collection is returned to the caller to ensure that the execution log
576: // is printed after the nodes are created.
577: // Nodes interdependencies are created in this function.
578: func (c *CLab) createNodes(
579: 	ctx context.Context,
580: 	maxWorkers uint,
581: 	skipPostDeploy bool,
582: ) (*sync.WaitGroup, *clabexec.ExecCollection, chan error, error) {
583: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
584: 		c.dependencyManager.AddNode(node)
585: 	}
586: 
587: 	// nodes with static mgmt IP should be scheduled before the dynamic ones
588: 	err := c.createStaticDynamicDependency()
589: 	if err != nil {
590: 		return nil, nil, nil, err
591: 	}
592: 
593: 	// create user-defined node dependencies done with `wait-for` property of the deployment stage
594: 	err = c.createWaitForDependency()
595: 	if err != nil {
596: 		return nil, nil, nil, err
597: 	}
598: 
599: 	// make network namespace shared containers start in the right order
600: 	c.createNamespaceSharingDependency()
601: 
602: 	// Add possible additional dependencies here
603: 
604: 	// make sure that there are no unresolvable dependencies, which would deadlock.
605: 	err = c.dependencyManager.CheckAcyclicity()
606: 	if err != nil {
607: 		return nil, nil, nil, err
608: 	}
609: 
610: 	// Buffered so workers never block reporting a failure (at most one failure path per node).
611: 	// With zero nodes, nothing sends on this channel; an empty buffer is fine.
612: 	nodeFailCh := make(chan error, len(c.Nodes))
613: 
614: 	// start scheduling
615: 	NodesWg, execCollection := c.scheduleNodes(ctx, int(maxWorkers), skipPostDeploy, nodeFailCh)
616: 
617: 	return NodesWg, execCollection, nodeFailCh, nil
618: }
619: 
620: // DeployNodes runs the node creation phase for the selected nodes.
621: func (c *CLab) DeployNodes(
622: 	ctx context.Context,
623: 	nodeNames []string,
624: 	maxWorkers uint,
625: ) error {
626: 	return c.deployNodes(ctx, nodeNames, maxWorkers, nil)
627: }
628: 
629: // deployApplyNodes schedules starts and creates together: a new sidecar can
630: // depend on a stopped target, and a stopped node can depend on a new target.
631: func (c *CLab) deployApplyNodes(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan, maxWorkers uint) error {
632: 	nodeNames := sortedStringSet(
633: 		unionStringSets(plan.addedNodeSet, plan.recreatedNodeSet, plan.startNodeSet),
634: 	)
635: 	return c.deployNodes(ctx, nodeNames, maxWorkers, plan.startNodeSet)
636: }
637: 
638: func (c *CLab) deployNodes(
639: 	ctx context.Context,
640: 	nodeNames []string,
641: 	maxWorkers uint,
642: 	startNodeSet map[string]struct{},
643: ) error {
644: 	if len(nodeNames) == 0 {
645: 		return nil
646: 	}
647: 
648: 	if maxWorkers == 0 || int(maxWorkers) > len(nodeNames) {
649: 		maxWorkers = uint(len(nodeNames))
650: 	}
651: 	if _, err := c.networkModeNodeOrder(nodeNames); err != nil {
652: 		return err
653: 	}
654: 
655: 	input := make(chan string)
656: 	errCh := make(chan error, len(nodeNames))
657: 	completed := make(map[string]*nodeDeployCompletion, len(nodeNames))
658: 	for _, name := range nodeNames {
659: 		completed[name] = &nodeDeployCompletion{done: make(chan struct{})}
660: 	}
661: 	finish := func(name string, err error) {
662: 		completed[name].err = err
663: 		close(completed[name].done)
664: 		errCh <- err
665: 	}
666: 
667: 	for range maxWorkers {
668: 		go func() {
669: 			for nodeName := range input {
670: 				if _, start := startNodeSet[nodeName]; start {
671: 					log.Info("Starting stopped node", "node", nodeName)
672: 					err := c.Nodes[nodeName].Start(ctx)
673: 					if err != nil {
674: 						err = fmt.Errorf("failed starting node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
675: 					}
676: 					finish(nodeName, err)
677: 					continue
678: 				}
679: 				log.Info("Creating node", "node", nodeName)
680: 				finish(nodeName, c.deployNode(ctx, c.Nodes[nodeName]))
681: 			}
682: 		}()
683: 	}
684: 
685: 	// Wait outside the worker pool. Targets in this operation signal completion
686: 	// directly, avoiding runtime polling and propagating failures to dependents.
687: 	var feedWg sync.WaitGroup
688: 	for _, nodeName := range nodeNames {
689: 		feedWg.Add(1)
690: 		go func(nodeName string) {
691: 			defer feedWg.Done()
692: 			if err := c.waitForNodeDeployTarget(ctx, nodeName, completed); err != nil {
693: 				finish(nodeName, err)
694: 				return
695: 			}
696: 			select {
697: 			case input <- nodeName:
698: 			case <-ctx.Done():
699: 				finish(nodeName, ctx.Err())
700: 			}
701: 		}(nodeName)
702: 	}
703: 	go func() {
704: 		feedWg.Wait()
705: 		close(input)
706: 	}()
707: 
708: 	var errs []error
709: 	for range nodeNames {
710: 		if err := <-errCh; err != nil {
711: 			errs = append(errs, err)
712: 		}
713: 	}
714: 
715: 	if len(errs) > 0 {
716: 		return errors.Join(errs...)
717: 	}
718: 
719: 	return nil
720: }
721: 
722: type nodeDeployCompletion struct {
723: 	done chan struct{}
724: 	// Closing done publishes err to dependent goroutines.
725: 	err error
726: }
727: 
728: func (c *CLab) waitForNodeDeployTarget(
729: 	ctx context.Context,
730: 	name string,
731: 	completed map[string]*nodeDeployCompletion,
732: ) error {
733: 	if err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {
734: 		return err
735: 	}
736: 	target := networkModeContainerTarget(c.Nodes[name].Config().NetworkMode)
737: 	if target == "" {
738: 		target = c.Nodes[name].Config().Labels[clabnodestailscale.ParentLabel]
739: 	}
740: 	if completion, selected := completed[target]; selected {
741: 		select {
742: 		case <-completion.done:
743: 			if completion.err != nil {
744: 				return fmt.Errorf(
745: 					"node %q depends on failed node %q: %w",
746: 					name,
747: 					target,
748: 					completion.err,
749: 				)
750: 			}
751: 			return nil
752: 		case <-ctx.Done():
753: 			return ctx.Err()
754: 		}
755: 	}
756: 	return c.waitForApplyNetworkModeTarget(ctx, name)
757: }
758: 
759: // waitForApplyNetworkModeTarget waits until a node's network-mode:
760: // container:<target> target is running, so apply's incremental deploy never
761: // races a newly created or recreated target against its dependent. The
762: // target may be a node in this same apply call (added or recreated
763: // alongside the dependent) or, like fresh deploy, an external container not
764: // managed by this topology.
765: func (c *CLab) waitForApplyNetworkModeTarget(ctx context.Context, nodeName string) error {
766: 	node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
767: 	if !exists {
768: 		return nil
769: 	}
770: 
771: 	target := networkModeContainerTarget(node.Config().NetworkMode)
772: 	if target == "" {
773: 		return nil
774: 	}
775: 
776: 	targetNode, internal := c.Nodes[target]
777: 	if !internal {
778: 		return c.waitForExternalNodeDependencies(ctx, nodeName)
779: 	}
780: 
781: 	runtime := c.globalRuntime()
782: 	if runtime == nil {
783: 		return fmt.Errorf("container runtime is not initialized")
784: 	}
785: 
786: 	return clabruntime.WaitForContainerRunning(ctx, runtime, targetNode.Config().LongName, nodeName)
787: }
788: 
789: func (c *CLab) deployNode(ctx context.Context, node clabnodes.Node) error {
790: 	nodeName := node.GetShortName()
791: 	if err := node.PreDeploy(ctx, &clabnodes.PreDeployParams{
792: 		Cert:         c.Cert,
793: 		TopologyName: c.Config.Name,
794: 		TopoPaths:    c.TopoPaths,
795: 		SSHPubKeys:   c.SSHPubKeys,
796: 	}); err != nil {
797: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q pre-deploy: %w", nodeName, err)
798: 	}
799: 
800: 	if err := node.Deploy(ctx, &clabnodes.DeployParams{Nodes: c.Nodes}); err != nil {
801: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q deploy: %w", nodeName, err)
802: 	}
803: 
804: 	if err := node.UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo(ctx); err != nil {
805: 		log.Errorf("failed to update node runtime information for node %s: %v", nodeName, err)
806: 	}
807: 
808: 	return nil
809: }
810: 
811: // DeployLinks deploys selected links through their endpoints and runs their post-deploy hooks.
812: func (c *CLab) DeployLinks(ctx context.Context, links []clablinks.Link) error {
813: 	return c.deployLinks(ctx, links, nil)
814: }
815: 
816: func (c *CLab) deployLinks(
817: 	ctx context.Context,
818: 	links []clablinks.Link,
819: 	additionalNodeNames []string,
820: ) error {
821: 	touchedNodes := make(map[string]struct{}, len(additionalNodeNames))
822: 	for _, nodeName := range additionalNodeNames {
823: 		touchedNodes[nodeName] = struct{}{}
824: 	}
825: 	for _, link := range links {
826: 		log.Info("Creating link", "link", applyLinkName(link))
827: 
828: 		for _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link) {
829: 			if err := ep.Deploy(ctx); err != nil {
830: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed deploying link %s: %w", applyLinkName(link), err)
831: 			}
832: 			touchedNodes[ep.GetNode().GetShortName()] = struct{}{}
833: 		}
834: 
835: 		if err := link.PostDeploy(ctx); err != nil {
836: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed post-deploying link %s: %w", applyLinkName(link), err)
837: 		}
838: 	}
839: 
840: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedStringSet(touchedNodes) {
841: 		node, exists := c.Nodes[nodeName]
842: 		if !exists {
843: 			continue
844: 		}
845: 		if err := node.PostDeployEndpoints(ctx); err != nil {
846: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q post-deploy links: %w", nodeName, err)
847: 		}
848: 	}
849: 
850: 	return nil
851: }
852: 
853: func (c *CLab) postDeployApplyNodes(
854: 	ctx context.Context,
855: 	nodeNames []string,
856: 	skipPostDeploy bool,
857: ) error {
858: 	execCollection := clabexec.NewExecCollection()
859: 
860: 	for _, nodeName := range nodeNames {
861: 		node := c.Nodes[nodeName]
862: 
863: 		if !skipPostDeploy {
864: 			if err := node.PostDeploy(
865: 				ctx,
866: 				&clabnodes.PostDeployParams{Nodes: c.Nodes},
867: 			); err != nil {
868: 				return fmt.Errorf("node %q post-deploy: %w", nodeName, err)
869: 			}
870: 		}
871: 
872: 		if err := node.RunExecFromConfig(ctx, execCollection); err != nil {
873: 			log.Errorf("failed to run exec commands for %s: %v", nodeName, err)
874: 		}
875: 	}
876: 
877: 	execCollection.Log()
878: 
879: 	return nil
880: }
881: 
882: func (c *CLab) finalize(
883: 	ctx context.Context,
884: 	exportTemplate string,
885: 	graph bool,
886: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
887: 	if err := c.GenerateInventories(); err != nil {
888: 		return nil, err
889: 	}
890: 
891: 	topoDataF, err := os.Create(c.TopoPaths.TopoExportFile())
892: 	if err != nil {
893: 		return nil, err
894: 	}
895: 	defer topoDataF.Close()
896: 
897: 	if err := c.GenerateExports(ctx, topoDataF, exportTemplate); err != nil {
898: 		return nil, err
899: 	}
900: 
901: 	if graph {
902: 		if err := c.GenerateDotGraph(ctx); err != nil {
903: 			log.Error(err)
904: 		}
905: 	}
906: 
907: 	containers, err := c.ListNodesContainers(ctx)
908: 	if err != nil {
909: 		return nil, err
910: 	}
911: 
912: 	if !c.mgmtNetworksSkipped() {
913: 		log.Info("Adding host entries", "path", "/etc/hosts")
914: 		if err := c.appendHostsFileEntries(ctx); err != nil {
915: 			log.Errorf("failed to create hosts file: %v", err)
916: 		}
917: 	}
918: 
919: 	log.Info("Adding SSH config for nodes", "path", c.TopoPaths.SSHConfigPath())
920: 	if err := c.addSSHConfig(); err != nil {
921: 		log.Errorf("failed to create ssh config file: %v", err)
922: 	}
923: 
924: 	c.writeState()
925: 
926: 	return containers, nil
927: }
928: 
929: func (c *CLab) writeState() {
930: 	if err := c.WriteState(); err != nil {
931: 		log.Warnf("failed to write state file: %v", err)
932: 	}
933: }
934: 
935: // configureSnapshotRestore configures nodes for snapshot restoration.
936: // It resolves snapshot files for each node and adds the necessary volume mounts
937: // and environment variables to restore from snapshots.
938: func (c *CLab) configureSnapshotRestore(options *DeployOptions) error {
939: 	// Build restore map from per-node specifications
940: 	restoreMap := make(map[string]string)
941: 	for _, mapping := range options.restoreNodeSnapshots {
942: 		parts := strings.SplitN(mapping, "=", 2)
943: 		if len(parts) != 2 {
944: 			return fmt.Errorf("invalid restore format: %s (expected: node=path)", mapping)
945: 		}
946: 		restoreMap[parts[0]] = parts[1]
947: 	}
948: 
949: 	// Track statistics for logging
950: 	var restoredCount, freshCount int
951: 
952: 	// Configure each node
953: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
954: 		nodeName := node.Config().ShortName
955: 
956: 		// Resolve snapshot path for this node
957: 		snapshotPath, shouldRestore := resolveNodeSnapshot(nodeName, restoreMap, options.restoreAll)
958: 
959: 		if shouldRestore {
960: 			// Validate snapshot file exists
961: 			if _, err := os.Stat(snapshotPath); os.IsNotExist(err) {
962: 				return fmt.Errorf("snapshot file not found for node %s: %s", nodeName, snapshotPath)
963: 			}
964: 
965: 			// Get absolute path for bind mount
966: 			absPath, err := filepath.Abs(snapshotPath)
967: 			if err != nil {
968: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed to get absolute path for %s: %w", snapshotPath, err)
969: 			}
970: 
971: 			// Add volume mount for snapshot (read-only)
972: 			node.Config().Binds = append(node.Config().Binds,
973: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s:/snapshot.tar:ro", absPath))
974: 
975: 			// Add restore environment variable
976: 			if node.Config().Env == nil {
977: 				node.Config().Env = make(map[string]string)
978: 			}
979: 			node.Config().Env["RESTORE_SNAPSHOT"] = "1"
980: 
981: 			log.Infof("Node %s will restore from: %s", nodeName, snapshotPath)
982: 			restoredCount++
983: 		} else {
984: 			freshCount++
985: 		}
986: 	}
987: 
988: 	// Log deployment mode summary if any restores are configured
989: 	if restoredCount > 0 {
990: 		log.Infof("Deploying %d nodes from snapshots, %d nodes fresh", restoredCount, freshCount)
991: 	}
992: 
993: 	return nil
994: }
995: 
996: // resolveNodeSnapshot determines the snapshot file path for a given node.
997: // Priority: per-node override > restore-all directory > none.
998: func resolveNodeSnapshot(
999: 	nodeName string,
1000: 	restoreMap map[string]string,
1001: 	restoreAll string,
1002: ) (string, bool) {
1003: 	// 1. Check per-node overrides first (highest priority)
1004: 	if snapshotPath, ok := restoreMap[nodeName]; ok {
1005: 		return snapshotPath, true
1006: 	}
1007: 
1008: 	// 2. Check restore-all directory
1009: 	if restoreAll != "" {
1010: 		// Look for {nodename}.tar in directory
1011: 		snapshotPath := filepath.Join(restoreAll, nodeName+".tar")
1012: 		if _, err := os.Stat(snapshotPath); err == nil {
1013: 			return snapshotPath, true
1014: 		}
1015: 
1016: 		// Not found - this is OK, just deploy normally
1017: 		log.Debugf("No snapshot found for node %s in %s, deploying normally", nodeName, restoreAll)
1018: 		return "", false
1019: 	}
1020: 
1021: 	// 3. No restore specified
1022: 	return "", false
1023: }

(End of file - total 1023 lines)

{\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"dry-run cannot be combined with reconfigure: \" +\n\t\t\t\t\t\"reconfigure always destroys and redeploys the full lab\",\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tcontainers, {\n\t\tplanner, ok := c.LabRuntime.(clablabruntime.TopologyPlanner)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"lab runtime %q does not support dry-run\", c.globalRuntimeName)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treq, err := c.labRuntimeDeployRequest()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tapplyLabRuntimeDeployOptions(&req, options)\n\n\t\tplan, err := planner.Plan(ctx, req)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn &DeployResult{RuntimePlan: plan}, nil\n\t}\n\n\tcurrentNodes, err := c.runtimeNodeGroups(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tinitialDeploy, err := c.needsInitialDeploy(currentNodes)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tif initialDeploy {\n\t\tif options.dryRun {\n\t\t\treturn &DeployResult{\n\t\t\t\tApply: &ApplyResult{\n\t\t\t\t\tDryRun:      true,\n\t\t\t\t\tDeployedLab: true,\n\t\t\t\t\tLabName:     c.Config.Name,\n\t\t\t\t},\n\t\t\t}, nil\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tcontainers, err := c.deploy(ctx, options)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn &DeployResult{Containers: containers}, nil\n\t}\n\n\tif err := c.checkReconcileDeployOptions(options); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tapplyOptions := &ApplyOptions{\n\t\tdryRun:             options.dryRun,\n\t\tskipPostDeploy:     options.skipPostDeploy,\n\t\tskipLabDirFileACLs: options.skipLabDirFileACLs,\n\t\tgraph:              options.graph,\n\t\tfinalizeNoop:       true,\n\t\tmaxWorkers:         options.maxWorkers,\n\t\texportTemplate:     options.exportTemplate,\n\t}\n\tapplyResult, err := c.apply(ctx, applyOptions, currentNodes)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif options.dryRun {\n\t\treturn &DeployResult{Apply: applyResult}, nil\n\t}\n\n\tcontainers, err := c.ListNodesContainers(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn &DeployResult{Containers: containers, Apply: applyResult}, nil\n}\n\n// checkReconcileDeployOptions rejects deploy options that only make sense for a fresh\n// deployment when deploy is about to reconcile an already deployed lab.\nfunc (c *CLab) checkReconcileDeployOptions(options *DeployOptions) error {\n\tif c.managementNetworkOverridden {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"management network overrides require a fresh deployment, but lab %q is already \"+\n\t\t\t\t\"deployed; use --reconfigure to destroy and redeploy it\",\n\t\t\tc.Config.Name,\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\tif options.restoreAll != \"\" || len(options.restoreNodeSnapshots) > 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"snapshot restore requires a fresh deployment, but lab %q is already deployed; \"+\n\t\t\t\t\"use --reconfigure to destroy and redeploy it\",\n\t\t\tc.Config.Name,\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\tif len(c.nodeFilter) > 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"node filter is not supported when reconciling deployed lab %q: \"+\n\t\t\t\t\"running nodes excluded by the filter would be deleted; \"+\n\t\t\t\t\"remove the filter or use --reconfigure\",\n\t\t\tc.Config.Name,\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// deploy creates a lab that has no managed runtime nodes yet.\n// skipcq: GO-R1005\nfunc (c *CLab) deploy( //nolint: funlen\n\tctx context.Context,\n\toptions *DeployOptions,\n) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {\n\tif c.LabRuntime != nil {\n\t\treturn c.deployWithLabRuntime(ctx, options)\n\t}\n\n\tvar err error\n\n\terr = c.ResolveLinks()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Debugf(\"lab Conf: %+v\", c.Config)\n\n\tif options.reconfigure {\n\t\t_ = c.destroy(ctx, uint(len(c.Nodes)), true)\n\t\tlog.Info(\"Removing directory\", \"path\", c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir())\n\n\t\tif err := c.checkTopologyDefinition(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif err := c.prepareDeployArtifacts(ctx, options.skipLabDirFileACLs); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\t// Apply snapshot restore configuration to nodes\n\tif err := c.configureSnapshotRestore(options); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tnodesWg, execCollection, nodeFailCh, err := c.createNodes(\n\t\tctx,\n\t\toptions.maxWorkers,\n\t\toptions.skipPostDeploy,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif err := waitForNodeDeploy(ctx, nodesWg, nodeFailCh); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tif err := c.syncTailscaleProxy(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tif err := c.SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\t// also call deploy on the special nodes endpoints (only host is required for the\n\t// vxlan stitched endpoints).\n\t// this must happen after all node workers have finished so that veth pairs created\n\t// as part of vxlan-stitch links are already deployed before the stitch TC rules are applied.\n\teps := c.getSpecialLinkNodes()[\"host\"].GetEndpoints()\n\tfor _, ep := range eps {\n\t\terr = ep.Deploy(ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tlog.Warnf(\"failed deploying endpoint %s\", ep)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Stitch links after node workers and host endpoints have finished.\n\t// The veth pair is already created by node workers and the VxLAN interface is created\n\t// by host endpoint deploy; Stitch applies the TC redirect rules to bridge them.\n\tvar linkPostDeployWorkers errgroup.Group\n\tlinkPostDeployWorkers.SetLimit(int(options.maxWorkers))\n\tfor _, link := range c.Links {\n\t\tlinkPostDeployWorkers.Go(func() error {\n\t\t\tif err := link.PostDeploy(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tlog.Warnf(\"failed post-deploying link: %v\", err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\treturn nil\n\t\t})\n\t}\n\t_ = linkPostDeployWorkers.Wait()\n\n\texecCollection.Log()\n\n\treturn c.finalize(ctx, options.exportTemplate, options.graph)\n}\n\nfunc waitForNodeDeploy(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\tnodesWg *sync.WaitGroup,\n\tnodeFailCh chan error,\n) error {\n\tif nodesWg != nil {\n\t\tnodesWg.Wait()\n\t}\n\n\tclose(nodeFailCh)\n\n\t// Cancellation takes precedence over errors caused by workers unwinding, and\n\t// prevents post-deploy work from running against a partial lab.\n\tif err := ctx.Err(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tvar nodeFailErrs []error\n\tfor nodeErr := range nodeFailCh {\n\t\tnodeFailErrs = append(nodeFailErrs, nodeErr)\n\t}\n\tif len(nodeFailErrs) > 0 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"deployment failed for one or more nodes: %w\",\n\t\t\terrors.Join(nodeFailErrs...),\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// prepareLabManagementNetwork creates the management networks that are in use\n// and allocates addresses for the nodes attached to them.\nfunc (c *CLab) prepareLabManagementNetwork(\n\tctx != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\treturn c.setMgmtNetLinkBridges()\n}\n\nfunc (c *CLab) allocateLabManagementIPs(\n\tctx context.Context,\n\texisting []clabtypes.ExistingAddress,\n) error {\n\tpreferred := make(map[string]clabtypes.NodeAddresses)\n\tstate, err := c.LoadState()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Warn(\"Ignoring preferred management addresses\", \"error\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tif {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tnodes := c.mgmtNetNodes(m)\n\t\tif m.IPAM.Provider == clabtypes.IPAMProviderRuntime || len(nodes) == 0 {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treserved, err := &&\n\t\t\treused[ownerAddress{entry.ContainerID, entry.Address}] {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\treserved = append(reserved, entry.Address)\n\t}\n\treturn reserved, nil\n}\n\nfunc (c *CLab) prepareDeployArtifacts(ctx context.Context, skipLabDirFileACLs bool) error {\n\tif err := c.loadKernelModules(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\treturn f.Close()\n}\n\nfunc (c *CLab) setupNodeAuth(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tif err := c.certificateAuthoritySetup(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tvar err make([]string, 0, len(c.Nodes))\n\n\tfor _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {\n\t\tn := c.Nodes[nodeName]\n\t\tif n.Config().MgmtIPv4Address != \"\" {\n\t\t\tlog.Debugf(\"Adding static ipv4 /etc/hosts entry for %s:%s\",\n\t\t\t\tn.Config().ShortName, n.Config().MgmtIPv4Address)\n\t\t\textraHosts = append(extraHosts, n.Config().ShortName+\":\"+n.Config().MgmtIPv4Address)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif
